          <p class="in">(b) An eligible energy technology may blend or co-fire a fuel listed in subdivision 1, paragraph (c), clause (5), with other fuels in the generation facility, but only the percentage of electricity that is attributable to a fuel listed in that clause can be counted toward an electric utility's standard obligation under subdivision 2a.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1691.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p class="in">[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/3/#laws.0.1.0">2007 c 3 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1691.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Compliance.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commission must regularly investigate whether an electric utility is in compliance with the electric utility's standard obligation under subdivisions 2a, 2f, and 2g. If the commission finds noncompliance, it may order the electric utility to construct facilities, purchase energy generated by eligible energy technology, purchase renewable energy credits, or engage in other activities to achieve compliance. If an electric utility fails to comply with an order under this subdivision, the commission may impose a financial penalty on the electric utility in an amount not to exceed the estimated cost of the electric utility to achieve compliance. The penalty may not exceed the lesser of the cost of constructing facilities or purchasing credits. The commission must deposit financial penalties imposed under this subdivision in the energy and conservation account established in the special revenue fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241#stat.216B.241.2a">216B.241, subdivision 2a</a>. This subdivision is in addition to and does not limit any other authority of the commission to enforce this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1691.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Relation to other law.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">This section does not limit the authority of the commission under any other law, including, without limitation, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2422">216B.2422</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.243">216B.243</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1691.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Local benefits.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commission shall take all reasonable actions within the commission's statutory authority to ensure this section is implemented in a manner that maximizes net benefits to all Minnesota citizens. Reasonable actions the commission must take and benefits that must be maximized include but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) the creation of high-quality jobs in Minnesota paying wages that support families;</p>
          <p>(2) recognition of the rights of workers to organize and unionize;</p>
          <p>(3) ensuring that workers have the necessary tools, opportunities, and economic assistance to adapt successfully during the energy transition, particularly in environmental justice areas;</p>
          <p>(4) ensuring that all Minnesotans share (i) the benefits of clean and renewable energy, and (ii) the opportunity to participate fully in the clean energy economy;</p>
          <p>(5) ensuring that statewide air emissions are reduced, particularly in environmental justice areas; and</p>
          <p>(6) the provision of affordable electric service to Minnesotans, particularly to low-income consumers.</p>
          <p>(b) The commission must also implement this section in a manner that balances factors such as local ownership of or participation in energy production, development and ownership of eligible energy technology facilities by independent power producers, Minnesota utility ownership of eligible energy technology facilities, the costs of energy generation to satisfy the renewable and carbon-free standards, and the reliability of electric service to Minnesotans.</p>
          <p>(c) When making investments to meet the requirements under this section, utilities are encouraged to locate new energy generating facilities in Minnesota communities where fossil-fuel-generating plants have been retired or are scheduled for retirement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1691.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Utility acquisition of resources.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A competitive resource acquisition process established by the commission prior to June 1, 2007, shall not apply to a utility for the construction, ownership, and operation of generation facilities used to satisfy the requirements of this section unless, upon a finding that it is in the public interest, the commission issues an order on or after June 1, 2007, that requires compliance by a utility with a competitive resource acquisition process. A utility that owns a nuclear generation facility and intends to construct, own, or operate facilities under this section shall file with the commission as part of the utility's filing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2422">216B.2422</a> a renewable energy plan setting forth the manner in which the utility proposes to meet the requirements of this section. The commission shall approve the plan unless it determines, after public hearing and comment, that the plan is not in the public interest. As part of its determination of public interest, the commission shall consider the plan's impact on balancing the state's interest in:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) promoting the policy of economic development in rural areas through the development of renewable energy projects, as expressed in subdivision 9;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) maintaining the reliability of the state's electric power grid; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) minimizing cost impacts on ratepayers.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.1691.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/212/#laws.8.3.0">2001 c 212 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/398/#laws.0.3.0">2002 c 398 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/11/#laws.2.3.0">1Sp2003 c 11 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/3/#laws.0.1.0">2007 c 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/136/#laws.4.10.0">2007 c 136 art 4 s 10</a>; art 6 s 1,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/258/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 258 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/110/#laws.0.13.0">2009 c 110 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.45.0">2010 c 382 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.30.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/97/#laws.0.14.0">2011 c 97 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.7.3.0">2013 c 85 art 7 s 3</a>; art 10 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.6.6.0">2016 c 189 art 6 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.10.9.0">2017 c 94 art 10 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/193/#laws.0.2.0">2018 c 193 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/4/#laws.8.16.0">1Sp2021 c 4 art 8 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/7/#laws.0.3.0">2023 c 7 s 3</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.12.16.0">2023 c 60 art 12 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.1692">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.1692 EMISSIONS-REDUCTION RIDER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1692.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Qualifying projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Projects that may be approved for the emissions reduction-rate rider allowed in this section must:</p>
          <p>(1) be installed on existing large electric generating power plants, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2421#stat.216B.2421.2">216B.2421, subdivision 2, clause (1)</a>, that are located in the state and that are currently not subject to emissions limitations for new power plants under the federal Clean Air Act, United States Code, title 42, section 7401 et seq.;</p>
          <p>(2) not increase the capacity of the existing electric generating power plant more than ten percent or more than 100 megawatts, whichever is greater; and</p>
          <p>(3) result in the existing plant either:</p>
          <p>(i) complying with applicable new source review standards under the federal Clean Air Act; or</p>
          <p>(ii) emitting air contaminants at levels substantially lower than allowed for new facilities by the applicable new source performance standards under the federal Clean Air Act; or</p>
          <p>(iii) reducing emissions from current levels at a unit to the lowest cost-effective level when, due to the age or condition of the generating unit, the public utility demonstrates that it would not be cost-effective to reduce emissions to the levels in item (i) or (ii).</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), a project may be approved for the emission reduction rate rider allowed in this section if the project is to be installed on existing large electric generating power plants, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2421#stat.216B.2421.2">216B.2421, subdivision 2</a>, clause (1), that are located outside the state and are needed to comply with state or federal air quality standards, but only if the project has received an advance determination of prudence from the commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.1695">216B.1695</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1692.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>Subdivisions 2, 4, and 5, paragraph (c), clause (1), do not apply to projects qualifying under subdivision 1, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1692.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Proposal submission.</span></h2>
          <p>A public utility that intends to submit a proposal for an emissions-reduction rider under this section must submit to the commission, the department, the Pollution Control Agency, and interested parties its plans for emissions-reduction projects at its generating facilities. This submission must be made at least 60 days in advance of a petition for a rider and shall include:</p>
          <p>(1) the priority order of emissions-reduction projects the utility plans to pursue at its generating facilities;</p>
          <p>(2) the planned schedule for implementation;</p>
          <p>(3) the analysis and considerations relied on by the public utility to develop that priority ranking;</p>
          <p>(4) the alternative emissions-reduction projects considered, including but not limited to applications of the best available control technology and repowering with natural gas, and reasons for not pursuing them;</p>
          <p>(5) the emissions reductions expected to be achieved by the projects and their relation to applicable standards for new facilities under the federal Clean Air Act, United States Code, title 42, section 7401 et seq.; and</p>
          <p>(6) the general rationale and conclusions of the public utility in determining the priority ranking.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1692.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Filing petition to recover project costs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A public utility may petition the commission for approval of an emissions-reduction rider to recover the costs of a qualifying emissions-reduction project outside of a general rate case proceeding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.16">216B.16</a>. In its filing, the public utility shall provide:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of the planned emissions-reduction project;</p>
          <p>(2) the activities involved in the project;</p>
          <p>(3) a schedule for implementation;</p>
          <p>(4) any analysis provided to the Pollution Control Agency regarding the project;</p>
          <p>(5) an assessment of alternatives to the project, including costs, environmental impact, and operational issues;</p>
          <p>(6) the proposed method of cost recovery;</p>
          <p>(7) any proposed recovery above cost; and</p>
          <p>(8) the projected emissions reductions from the project.</p>
          <p>(b) Nothing in this section precludes a public utility or interested party from seeking commission guidelines for emissions-reduction rider filings; however, commission guidelines are not required as a prerequisite to a public utility-initiated filing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1692.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Environmental assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>The Pollution Control Agency shall evaluate the public utility's emissions-reduction project filing and provide the commission with:</p>
          <p>(1) verification that the emissions-reduction project qualifies under subdivision 1;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the projected environmental benefits of the proposed project; and</p>
          <p>(3) its assessment of the appropriateness of the proposed project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1692.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Proposal approval.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) After receiving the Pollution Control Agency's environmental assessment, the commission shall allow opportunity for written and oral comment on the proposed emissions reduction-rate rider proposal. The commission must assess the costs of an emissions-reduction project on a stand-alone basis and may approve, modify, or reject the proposed emissions-reduction rider. In making its determination, the commission shall consider whether the project, proposed cost recovery, and any proposed recovery above cost appropriately achieves environmental benefits without unreasonable consumer costs.</p>
          <p>(b) The commission may approve a rider that:</p>
          <p>(1) allows the utility to recover costs of qualifying emissions-reduction projects net of revenues attributable to the project;</p>
          <p>(2) allows an appropriate return on investment associated with qualifying emissions-reduction projects at the level established in the public utility's last general rate case;</p>
          <p>(3) allocates project costs appropriately between wholesale and retail customers;</p>
          <p>(4) provides a mechanism for recovery above cost, if necessary to improve the overall economics of the qualifying projects to ensure implementation;</p>
          <p>(5) recovers costs from retail customer classes in proportion to class energy consumption; and</p>
          <p>(6) terminates recovery once the costs of qualifying projects have been fully recovered.</p>
          <p>(c) The commission must not approve an emissions-reduction project and its associated rate rider if:</p>
          <p>(1) the emissions-reduction project is needed to comply with new state or federal air quality standards; or</p>
          <p>(2) the emissions-reduction project is required as a corrective action as part of any state or federal enforcement action.</p>
          <p>(d) The commission may not include any costs of a proposed project in the emissions-reduction rider that are not directly allocable to reduction of emissions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1692.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Implementation.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 60 days of a final commission order, the public utility shall notify the commission and the Pollution Control Agency whether it will proceed with the project. Nothing in this section commits a public utility to implementing a proposed emissions-reduction project if the proposed project or terms of the emissions-reduction rider have been either modified or rejected by the commission. A public utility implementing a project under this section will not be required for a period of eight years after installation to undertake additional investments to comply with a new state requirement regarding pollutants addressed by the project at the project generating facility. This section does not affect requirements of federal law. The term of the rider shall extend for the period approved by the commission regardless of any subsequent state or federal requirement affecting any pollutant addressed by the approved emissions-reduction project and regardless of the sunset date in subdivision 8.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1692.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Evaluation and report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15, 2005, the commission, in consultation with the commissioner of commerce and commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, shall report to the legislature:</p>
          <p>(1) the number of participating public utilities and qualifying projects proposed and approved under this section;</p>
          <p>(2) the total cost of each project and any associated incentives;</p>
          <p>(3) the reduction in air emissions achieved;</p>
          <p>(4) rate impacts of the cost recovery mechanisms; and</p>
          <p>(5) an assessment of the effectiveness of the cost recovery mechanism in accomplishing power plant emissions reductions in excess of those required by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1692.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Sunset.</span></h2>
          <p>This section is effective until December 31, 2020, and applies to plans, projects, and riders approved before that date and modifications made to them after that date.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.1692.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.3.12.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/201/#laws.0.4.0">2006 c 201 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/325/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 325 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.31.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 31</a>,32; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.7.4.0">2013 c 85 art 7 s 4</a>-6</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.216B.1693"><b>216B.1693</b> MS 2010 [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/11/#laws.2.4.0">1Sp2003 c 11 art 2 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.1694">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.1694 INNOVATIVE ENERGY PROJECT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1694.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the term "innovative energy project" means a proposed energy-generation facility or group of facilities which may be located on up to three sites:</p>
          <p>(1) that makes use of an innovative generation technology utilizing coal as a primary fuel in a highly efficient combined-cycle configuration with significantly reduced sulfur dioxide, nitrogen oxide, particulate, and mercury emissions from those of traditional technologies;</p>
          <p>(2) that the project developer or owner certifies is a project capable of offering a long-term supply contract at a hedged, predictable cost; and</p>
          <p>(3) that is designated by the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation as a project that is located in the taconite tax relief area on a site that has substantial real property with adequate infrastructure to support new or expanded development and that has received prior financial and other support from the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1694.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Regulatory incentives.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An innovative energy project:</p>
          <p>(1) is exempted from the requirements for a certificate of need under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.243">216B.243</a>, for the generation facilities, and transmission infrastructure associated with the generation facilities, but is subject to all applicable environmental review and permitting procedures of chapter 216E;</p>
          <p>(2) once permitted and constructed, is eligible to increase the capacity of the associated transmission facilities without additional state review upon filing notice with the commission;</p>
          <p>(3) has the power of eminent domain, which shall be limited to the sites and routes approved by the Environmental Quality Board for the project facilities. The project shall be considered a utility as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216I.02#stat.216I.02.19">216I.02, subdivision 19</a>, for the limited purpose of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216I.21">216I.21</a>. The project shall report any intent to exercise eminent domain authority to the board;</p>
          <p>(4) shall, prior to the approval by the commission of any arrangement to build or expand a fossil-fuel-fired generation facility, or to enter into an agreement to purchase capacity or energy from such a facility for a term exceeding five years, be considered as a supply option for the generation facility, and the commission shall ensure such consideration and take any action with respect to such supply proposal that it deems to be in the best interest of ratepayers;</p>
          <p>(5) shall make a good faith effort to secure funding from the United States Department of Energy and the United States Department of Agriculture to conduct a demonstration project at the facility for either geologic or terrestrial carbon sequestration projects to achieve reductions in facility emissions or carbon dioxide;</p>
          <p>(6) shall be entitled to enter into a contract with a public utility that owns a nuclear generation facility in the state to provide 450 megawatts of base-load capacity and energy under a long-term contract, subject to the approval of the terms and conditions of the contract by the commission. The commission may approve, disapprove, amend, or modify the contract in making its public interest determination, taking into consideration the project's economic development benefits to the state; the use of abundant domestic fuel sources; the stability of the price of the output from the project; the project's potential to contribute to a transition to hydrogen as a fuel resource; and the emissions reductions achieved compared to other solid fuel base-load technologies; and</p>
          <p>(7) shall be eligible for a grant from the renewable development account, subject to the approval of the entity administering that account, of $2,000,000 a year for five years for development and engineering costs, including those costs related to mercury-removal technology; thermal efficiency optimization and emission minimization; environmental impact statement preparation and licensing; development of hydrogen production capabilities; and fuel cell development and utilization.</p>
          <p>(b) This subdivision does not apply to nor affect a proposal to add utility-owned resources that is pending on May 29, 2003, before the Public Utilities Commission or to competitive bid solicitations to provide capacity or energy that is scheduled to be on line by December 31, 2006.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1694.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Staging and permitting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A natural gas-fired plant that is located on one site designated as an innovative energy project site under subdivision 1, clause (3), is accorded the regulatory incentives granted to an innovative energy project under subdivision 2, clauses (1) to (3), and may exercise the authorities therein.</p>
          <p>(b) Following issuance of a final state or federal environmental impact statement for an innovative energy project that was a subject of contested case proceedings before an administrative law judge:</p>
          <p>(1) site and route permits and water appropriation approvals for an innovative energy project must also be deemed valid for a plant meeting the requirements of paragraph (a) and shall remain valid until the later of (i) four years from the date the final required state or federal preconstruction permit is issued or (ii) June 30, 2025; and</p>
          <p>(2) no air, water, or other permit issued by a state agency that is necessary for constructing an innovative energy project may be the subject of contested case hearings, notwithstanding Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7000.1750">7000.1750</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7000.2200">7000.2200</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.1694.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/11/#laws.4.1.0">1Sp2003 c 11 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/97/#laws.0.16.0">2011 c 97 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/187/#laws.1.34.0">2012 c 187 art 1 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/94/#laws.7.15.0">2017 c 94 art 7 s 15</a>; art 10 s 10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/126/#laws.7.14.0">2024 c 126 art 7 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.43.14.0">2024 c 127 art 43 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.1695">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.1695 ENVIRONMENTAL PROJECTS; ADVANCE DETERMINATION OF PRUDENCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1695.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Qualifying project.</span></h2>
          <p>A public utility may petition the commission for an advance determination of prudence for a project undertaken to comply with federal or state air quality standards of states in which the utility's electric generation facilities are located, if the project has an expected jurisdictional cost to Minnesota ratepayers of at least $10,000,000. A project is undertaken to comply with federal or state air quality standards if it is required:</p>
          <p>(1) by the state in which the generation facility is located in a state implementation plan, permit, or order; or</p>
          <p>(2) to comply with section 111 or 112 of the federal Clean Air Act, United States Code, title 42, section 7411 or 7412.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1695.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Regulatory cost assessments and reports.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A utility requesting an advance determination under subdivision 1 must, as part of the evidence required when filing a petition under subdivision 3, provide to the Public Utilities Commission and the Pollution Control Agency an assessment of all anticipated state and federal environmental regulations related to the production of electricity from the utility's facility subject to the filing, including regulations relating to:</p>
          <p>(1) air pollution by nitrogen oxide and sulphur dioxide, including an assumption that Minnesota will be included in the federal Clean Air Interstate Rule region, hazardous air pollutants, carbon dioxide, particulates, and ozone;</p>
          <p>(2) coal waste; and</p>
          <p>(3) water consumption and water pollution.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition, the utility shall provide an assessment of the financial and operational impacts of these pending regulations applicable to the generating facility that is the subject of the filing and provide a range of regulatory response scenarios that include, but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) the installation of pollution control equipment;</p>
          <p>(2) the benefits of the retirement or repowering of the plant that is the subject of the filing with cleaner fuels considering the costs of complying with state and federal environmental regulations; and</p>
          <p>(3) the use of pollution allowances to achieve compliance.</p>
          <p>(c) The utility shall consult with interested stakeholders in establishing the scope of the regulatory, financial, and operational assessments prior to or during the 60-day period of the notice under subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1695.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Petition.</span></h2>
          <p>A petition filed under this section must include a description of the project, evidence supporting the project's reasonableness, a discussion of project alternatives, a project implementation schedule, a cost estimate and support for the reasonableness of the estimated cost, and a description of the public utility's efforts to ensure the lowest reasonable costs. Following receipt of the Pollution Control Agency's verification under subdivision 4, the commission shall allow opportunity for oral and written comment on the petition. The commission shall make a final determination on the petition within ten months of its filing date. The commission must make findings in support of its determination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1695.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Verification.</span></h2>
          <p>At least 60 days prior to filing a petition to the commission under subdivision 3, the utility shall file notice with the Pollution Control Agency that describes the project and how it qualifies under subdivision 1. The Pollution Control Agency shall, within 60 days of receipt of the notice, verify that the project qualifies under subdivision 1, and shall forward written verification to the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1695.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Cost recovery.</span></h2>
          <p>The utility may begin recovery of costs that have been incurred by the utility in connection with implementation of the project in the next rate case following an advance determination of prudence or in a rider approved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.1692">216B.1692</a>. The commission shall review the costs incurred by the utility for the project. The utility must show that the project costs are reasonable and necessary, and demonstrate its efforts to ensure the lowest reasonable project costs. Notwithstanding the commission's prior determination of prudence, it may accept, modify, or reject any of the project costs. The commission may determine whether to require an allowance for funds used during construction offset.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1695.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Rate of return.</span></h2>
          <p>The return on investment in the rider shall be at the level approved by the commission in the public utility's last general rate case, unless the commission determines that a different rate of return is in the public interest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1695.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Expiration.</span></h2>
          <p>A petition for an advance determination of prudence may not be filed after December 31, 2015.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.1695.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.5.7.0">2010 c 361 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/373/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 373 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.7.7.0">2013 c 85 art 7 s 7</a>,8</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.1696">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.1696 COMPETITIVE RATE FOR ENERGY-INTENSIVE, TRADE-EXPOSED ELECTRIC UTILITY CUSTOMER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1696.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Clean energy technology" is energy technology that generates electricity from a carbon neutral generating resource including, but not limited to, solar, wind, hydroelectric, and biomass.</p>
          <p>(c) "Energy-intensive trade-exposed customer" is defined to include:</p>
          <p>(1) an iron mining extraction and processing facility, including a scram mining facility as defined in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/6130.0100">6130.0100</a>, subpart 16;</p>
          <p>(2) a paper mill, wood products manufacturer, sawmill, or oriented strand board manufacturer;</p>
          <p>(3) a steel mill and related facilities; and</p>
          <p>(4) a retail customer of an investor-owned electric utility that has facilities under a single electric service agreement that: (i) collectively imposes a peak electrical demand of at least 10,000 kilowatts on the electric utility's system, (ii) has a combined annual average load factor in excess of 80 percent, and (iii) is subject to globally competitive pressures and whose electric energy costs are at least ten percent of the customer's overall cost of production.</p>
          <p>(d) "EITE rate schedule" means a rate schedule under which an investor-owned electric utility may set terms of service to an individual or group of energy-intensive trade-exposed customers.</p>
          <p>(e) "EITE rate" means the rate or rates offered by the investor-owned electric utility under an EITE rate schedule.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1696.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rates and terms of EITE rate schedule.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) It is the energy policy of the state of Minnesota to ensure competitive electric rates for energy-intensive trade-exposed customers. To achieve this objective, an investor-owned electric utility that has at least 50,000 retail electric customers, but no more than 200,000 retail electric customers, shall have the ability to propose various EITE rate options within their service territory under an EITE rate schedule that include, but are not limited to, fixed-rates, market-based rates, and rates to encourage utilization of new clean energy technology.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.03">216B.03</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.05">216B.05</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.06">216B.06</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.07">216B.07</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.16">216B.16</a>, the commission shall, upon a finding of net benefit to the utility or the state, approve an EITE rate schedule and any corresponding EITE rate.</p>
          <p>(c) The commission shall make a final determination in a proceeding begun under this section within 90 days of a miscellaneous rate filing by the electric utility.</p>
          <p>(d) Upon approval of any EITE rate schedule, the utility shall create a separate account to track the difference in revenue between what would have been collected under the electric utility's applicable standard tariff and the EITE rate schedule. In its next general rate case or through an EITE cost recovery rate rider between general rate cases, the commission shall allow the utility to recover any costs, including reduced revenues, or refund any savings, including increased revenues, associated with providing service to a customer under an EITE rate schedule. The utility shall not recover any costs or refund any savings under this section from any energy-intensive trade-exposed customer or any low-income residential ratepayers as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.16">216B.16</a>, subdivision 15.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1696.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Low-income funding.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the filing of a utility for approval of an EITE rate schedule under this section, the filing utility must deposit $10,000 into an account devoted to funding a program approved by the commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.16#stat.216B.16.15">216B.16, subdivision 15</a>. The funds shall be used to expand the outreach of the commission-approved affordability program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.1696.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of commerce shall assess reasonable costs it incurs for services it provides to implement this section to the utility proposing an EITE rate schedule to the commission. The department must not assess more than $854,000 per biennium under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.1696.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/1/#laws.3.26.0">1Sp2015 c 1 art 3 s 26</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COMPLAINTS AND HEARINGS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.17">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.17 COMPLAINT INVESTIGATION AND HEARING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Investigation.</span></h2>
          <p>On the commission's own motion or upon a complaint made against any public utility by the governing body of any political subdivision, by another public utility, by the department, by any 50 consumers of a particular utility, or by a complainant under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.172">216B.172</a> that any of the rates, tolls, tariffs, charges, or schedules or any joint rate or any regulation, measurement, practice, act, or omission affecting or relating to the production, transmission, delivery, or furnishing of natural gas or electricity or any service in connection therewith is in any respect unreasonable, insufficient, or unjustly discriminatory, or that any service is inadequate or cannot be obtained, the commission shall proceed, with notice, to make such investigation as it may deem necessary. The commission may dismiss any complaint without a hearing if in its opinion a hearing is not in the public interest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice of complaint.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall, prior to any formal hearing, notify the public utility complained of that a complaint has been made, and ten days after the notice has been given the commission may proceed to set a time and place for a hearing and an investigation as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice of hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall give the public utility and the complainant ten days' notice of the time and place when and where the hearing will be held and the matters to be considered and determined. Both the public utility and complainant are entitled to be heard and to be represented by counsel. A hearing under this section is not a contested case under chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Notice to local governments and interested persons.</span></h2>
          <p>Notice shall also be given to the governing bodies of affected municipalities and counties, and to any other persons the commission shall deem necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Combined notice.</span></h2>
          <p>The notice provided for in subdivisions 2 and 3 may be combined but if combined the notice shall not be less than ten days.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Complaint petition.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall have the power to hear, determine, and adjust complaints made against any municipally owned gas or electric utility with respect to rates and services upon petition of ten percent of the nonresident consumers of the municipally owned utility or 25 such nonresident consumers whichever is less. The hearing of the complaints shall be governed by this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Cooperative electric associations.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, public utility shall include cooperative electric associations with respect to service standards and practices only.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Evidence.</span></h2>
          <p>Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.60">14.60</a> shall be applicable to all contested cases before the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Further action by commission.</span></h2>
          <p>If after making an investigation under subdivision 1 and holding a hearing under this section, the commission finds that all significant factual issues raised have not been resolved to its satisfaction:</p>
          <p>(1) for investigations concerning the reasonableness of rates of a public utility, if the commission is unable to resolve the complaint with the utility, the commission may order the utility to initiate a rate proceeding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.16">216B.16</a>, provided, however, that the utility must be allowed at least 120 days after the date of the commission's order to initiate the proceeding; and</p>
          <p>(2) for investigations of other matters, the commission shall order that a contested case proceeding be conducted under chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.17.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Telecommunications and cable communications systems.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commission has authority under this section to investigate, upon complaint or on its own motion, conduct by or on behalf of a telecommunications carrier, telephone company, or cable communications system provider that impacts public utility or cooperative electric association infrastructure. If the commission finds that the conduct damaged or unreasonably interfered with the function of the infrastructure, the commission may take any action authorized under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.52">216B.52</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.61">216B.61</a> with respect to the provider.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision:</p>
          <p>(1) "telecommunications carrier" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.01">237.01, subdivision </a>6;</p>
          <p>(2) "telephone company" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.01#stat.237.01.7">237.01, subdivision 7</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) "cable communications system provider" means an owner or operator of a cable communications system, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/238.02#stat.238.02.3">238.02, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.17.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/429/#laws.0.17.0">1974 c 429 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/795/#laws.0.4.0">1978 c 795 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/614/#laws.0.113.0">1980 c 614 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/370/#laws.0.4.0">1990 c 370 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.12.17.0">2023 c 60 art 12 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.13.4.0">2024 c 127 art 13 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.172">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.172 CONSUMER DISPUTES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.172.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Appeal" means a request a complainant files with the commission to review and make a final decision regarding the resolution of the complainant's complaint by the consumer affairs office.</p>
          <p>(c) "Complainant" means an individual residential customer or a tenant who files with the consumer affairs office a complaint against a public utility or a landlord of a shared-metered residential building.</p>
          <p>(d) "Complaint" means an allegation submitted to the consumer affairs office by a complainant that a public utility's or a landlord's action or practice regarding billing or terms and conditions of service:</p>
          <p>(1) violates a statute, rule, tariff, service contract, or other provision of law;</p>
          <p>(2) is unreasonable; or</p>
          <p>(3) has harmed or, if not addressed, harms a complainant.</p>
          <p class="bl">Complaint does not include an objection to or a request to modify any natural gas or electricity rate contained in a tariff that has been approved by the commission. A complaint under this section is an informal complaint under Minnesota Rules, chapter 7829.</p>
          <p>(e) "Consumer affairs office" means the staff unit of the commission that is organized to receive and respond to complaints.</p>
          <p>(f) "Informal proceeding" has the meaning given in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7829.0100">7829.0100</a>, subpart 8.</p>
          <p>(g) "Landlord" means an owner of a shared-metered residential building, a contract for deed vendee, receiver, executor, trustee, lessee, agent, or other person directly or indirectly in control of the shared-metered residential building that bills separately for natural gas or electricity, or both.</p>
          <p>(h) "Public assistance" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/550.37#stat.550.37.14">550.37, subdivision 14</a>.</p>
          <p>(i) "Public utility" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.02#stat.216B.02.4">216B.02, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(j) "Shared-metered residential building" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.022">216B.022</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (e).</p>
          <p>(k) "Tenant" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.022#stat.216B.022.1">216B.022, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (g).</p>
          <p>(l) "Third-party billing agent" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.022">216B.022, subdivision </a>1, paragraph (h).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.172.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Complaint resolution procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>A complainant must first attempt to resolve a dispute with a public utility or a landlord. If a complainant is dissatisfied with the proposed resolution by the public utility or the landlord, the complainant may seek assistance of the commission to resolve the dispute by filing a complaint with the consumer affairs office. The consumer affairs office must: (1) notify the complainant of the resolution of the complaint; and (2) provide written notice of (i) the complainant's right to appeal the resolution to the commission, and (ii) the steps the complainant may take to appeal the resolution. Upon request, the consumer affairs office must provide to the complainant a written notice containing the substance of and basis for the resolution. Nothing in this section affects any other rights existing under this chapter or other law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.172.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appeal; final commission decision.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a complainant is not satisfied with the resolution of a complaint by the consumer affairs office, the complainant may file an appeal with the commission requesting that the commission make a final decision on the complaint. The commission's response to an appeal filed under this subdivision must comply with the notice requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.17">216B.17, subdivisions 2</a> to 5.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon the commission's receipt of an appeal filed under paragraph (a), the chair of the commission or a subcommittee delegated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216A.03#stat.216A.03.8">216A.03, subdivision 8</a>, to review the resolution of the complaint must decide whether the complaint be:</p>
          <p>(1) dismissed because there is no reasonable basis on which to proceed;</p>
          <p>(2) resolved through an informal commission proceeding; or</p>
          <p>(3) referred to the Office of Administrative Hearings for a contested case proceeding under chapter 14.</p>
          <p class="bl">A decision made under this paragraph must be provided in writing to the complainant and the public utility.</p>
          <p>(c) If the commission decides that the complaint be resolved through an informal proceeding before the commission or referred to the Office of Administrative Hearings for a contested case proceeding, the executive secretary must issue any procedural schedules, notices, or orders required to initiate an informal proceeding or a contested case.</p>
          <p>(d) The commission's dismissal of an appeal request or a decision rendered after conducting an informal proceeding is a final decision constituting an order or determination of the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.172.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Judicial review.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.27">216B.27</a>, a complainant may seek judicial review in district court of an adverse final decision under subdivision 3, paragraph (b), clause (1) or (2). Judicial review of the commission's decision in a contested case referred under subdivision 3, paragraph (b), clause (3), is governed by chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.172.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Right to service during pendency of dispute.</span></h2>
          <p>A public utility must continue or promptly restore service to a complainant during the pendency of an administrative or judicial procedure pursued by a complainant under this section, provided that the complainant:</p>
          <p>(1) agrees to enter into a payment agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.098#stat.216B.098.3">216B.098, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) posts the full disputed payment in escrow;</p>
          <p>(3) demonstrates receipt of public assistance or eligibility for legal aid services; or</p>
          <p>(4) demonstrates the complainant's household income is at or below 50 percent of the median income in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.172.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Rulemaking authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission may adopt rules to carry out the purposes of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.172.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.12.18.0">2023 c 60 art 12 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/107/#laws.0.5.0">2024 c 107 s 5</a>,6</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.18">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.18 SERVICE OF NOTICE.</h1>
        <p>Service of notice of all hearings, investigations, and proceedings pending before the commission and of complaints, reports, orders, and other documents must be made personally, by electronic service as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216.17">216.17</a>, or by mail as the commission may direct. Regulated utilities and state agencies must provide an electronic address to the commission for electronic service purposes and agree to accept electronic service as official service.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.18.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/429/#laws.0.18.0">1974 c 429 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/10/#laws.0.4.0">2007 c 10 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/135/#laws.3.20.0">2013 c 135 art 3 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.19">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.19 JOINT HEARING AND INVESTIGATION.</h1>
        <p>In the discharge of its duties under Laws 1974, chapter 429, the commission or the department may cooperate with similar commissions of other states and any federal agency and may hold joint hearings and make joint investigations with other commissions.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.19.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/429/#laws.0.19.0">1974 c 429 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/614/#laws.0.114.0">1980 c 614 s 114</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.20">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.20 SEPARATE RATE HEARING.</h1>
        <p>The commission may, in its discretion, when complaint is made of more than one rate or charge, order separate hearings thereon, and may consider and determine the several matters complained of separately and at times it may prescribe.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.20.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/429/#laws.0.20.0">1974 c 429 s 20</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.21">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.21 SUMMARY INVESTIGATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.21.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever the commission has reason to believe that any rate or charge may be unreasonable or unjustly discriminatory or that any service is inadequate or cannot be obtained or that an investigation of any matter relating to any public utility should for any reason be made, it may on its own motion summarily investigate the same with or without notice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.21.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Formal hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>If, after making the summary investigation, the commission becomes satisfied that sufficient grounds exist to warrant a formal hearing being ordered as to the matters investigated, it shall set a time and place for a hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.21.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice.</span></h2>
          <p>Notice of the time and place for the hearing shall be made as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.17">216B.17</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.18">216B.18</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.21.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/429/#laws.0.21.0">1974 c 429 s 21</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.22">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.22 MUNICIPALITY; AMICUS CURIAE AUTHORITY.</h1>
        <p>Any municipality that regulates and controls the exercise of a public utility franchise by reason of its home rule charter on January 1, 1975, is authorized to assist the Public Utilities Commission as amicus curiae in any proceeding brought before the commission with respect to the rates, fares, prices, regulation, or control of any utility operating therein.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.22.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/429/#laws.0.22.0">1974 c 429 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/614/#laws.0.123.0">1980 c 614 s 123</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.23">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.23 LAWFUL RATE; REASONABLE SERVICE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.23.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Determination as to rate; order.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever upon an investigation made under the provisions of Laws 1974, chapter 429, the commission shall find rates, tolls, charges, schedules or joint rates to be unjust, unreasonable, insufficient, or unjustly discriminatory or preferential or otherwise unreasonable or unlawful, the commission shall determine and by order fix reasonable rates, tolls, charges, schedules, or joint rates to be imposed, observed, and followed in the future in lieu of those found to be unreasonable or unlawful.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.23.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Authority to issue refund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) On determining that a public utility has charged a rate in violation of this chapter, a commission rule, or a commission order, the commission, after conducting a proceeding, may require the public utility to refund to its customers, in a manner approved by the commission, any revenues the commission finds were collected as a result of the unlawful conduct. Any refund authorized by this section is permitted in addition to any remedies authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.16">216B.16</a> or any other law governing rates. Exercising authority under this section does not preclude the commission from pursuing penalties under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.57">216B.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.61">216B.61</a> for the same conduct.</p>
          <p>(b) This section must not be construed as allowing:</p>
          <p>(1) retroactive ratemaking;</p>
          <p>(2) refunds based on claims that prior or current approved rates have been unjust, unreasonable, unreasonably preferential, discriminatory, insufficient, inequitable, or inconsistent in application to a class of customers; or</p>
          <p>(3) refunds based on claims that approved rates have not encouraged energy conservation or renewable energy use, or have not furthered the goals of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.164">216B.164</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241">216B.241</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216C.05">216C.05</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A refund under this subdivision does not apply to revenues collected more than six years before the date of the notice of the commission proceeding required under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.23.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Finding as to service; order.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever the commission shall find any regulations, measurements, practices, acts, or service to be unjust, unreasonable, insufficient, preferential, unjustly discriminatory, or otherwise unreasonable or unlawful, or shall find that any service which can be reasonably demanded cannot be obtained, the commission shall determine and by order fix reasonable measurements, regulations, acts, practices, or service to be furnished, imposed, observed and followed in the future in lieu of those found to be unreasonable, inadequate, or otherwise unlawful, and shall make any other order respecting the measurement, regulation, act, practice, or service as shall be just and reasonable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.23.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Copy of order served; notice.</span></h2>
          <p>A copy of the order shall be served upon the person against whom it runs or the person's attorney, and notice thereof shall be given to the other parties to the proceedings or their attorneys.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.23.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/429/#laws.0.23.0">1974 c 429 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/110/#laws.0.14.0">2009 c 110 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ENERGY CONSERVATION; UTILITY CONSTRUCTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.24">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.24 CONSTRUCTION OF MAJOR FACILITY; FILING PLANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.24.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Major utility facility defined.</span></h2>
          <p>The words "major utility facility" means: (1) electric generating plant and associated facilities designed for, or capable of, operation at a capacity of 50 megawatts or more; (2) an electric transmission line and associated facilities of a design capacity of 125 kilovolts or more; and (3) a gas transmission line and associated facilities designed for, or capable of, transporting gas at pressures in excess of 125 pounds per square inch; provided, however, that the words "major utility facility" shall not include electric or gas distribution lines and gas gathering lines and associated facilities as defined by the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.24.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Construction plan filed; rules.</span></h2>
          <p>Under rules as the commission may prescribe, every public utility shall file with the commission, within the time and in the form as the commission may designate, plans showing any contemplated construction of major utility facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.24.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Applicability to municipalities.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of this section shall apply to the construction of major utility facilities by a municipally owned gas or electric utility.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.24.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/429/#laws.0.24.0">1974 c 429 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.2401">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.2401 ENERGY SAVINGS AND OPTIMIZATION POLICY GOAL.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) The legislature finds that energy savings are an energy resource, and that cost-effective energy savings are preferred over all other energy resources. In addition, the legislature finds that optimizing the timing and method used by energy consumers to manage energy use provides significant benefits to the consumers and to the utility system as a whole. The legislature further finds that cost-effective energy savings and load management programs should be procured systematically and aggressively in order to reduce utility costs for businesses and residents, improve the competitiveness and profitability of businesses, create more energy-related jobs, reduce the economic burden of fuel imports, and reduce pollution and emissions that cause climate change. Therefore, it is the energy policy of the state of Minnesota to achieve annual energy savings equivalent to at least 2.5 percent of annual retail energy sales of electricity and natural gas through multiple measures, including but not limited to:</p>
        <p>(1) cost-effective energy conservation improvement programs and efficient fuel-switching utility programs under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2402">216B.2402</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241">216B.241</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) rate design;</p>
        <p>(3) energy efficiency achieved by energy consumers without direct utility involvement;</p>
        <p>(4) advancements in statewide energy codes and cost-effective appliance and equipment standards;</p>
        <p>(5) programs designed to transform the market or change consumer behavior;</p>
        <p>(6) energy savings resulting from efficiency improvements to the utility infrastructure and system; and</p>
        <p>(7) other efforts to promote energy efficiency and energy conservation.</p>
        <p>(b) A utility is encouraged to design and offer to customers load management programs that enable: (1) customers to maximize the economic value gained from the energy purchased from the customer's utility service provider; and (2) utilities to optimize the infrastructure and generation capacity needed to effectively serve customers and facilitate the integration of renewable energy into the energy system.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner must provide a reasonable estimate of progress made toward the statewide energy-savings goal under paragraph (a) in the annual report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241#stat.216B.241.1c">216B.241, subdivision 1c</a>, and make recommendations for administrative or legislative initiatives to increase energy savings toward that goal. The commissioner must also annually report on the energy productivity of the state's economy by estimating the ratio of economic output produced in the most recently completed calendar year to the primary energy inputs used in that year.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.216B.2401.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/136/#laws.2.4.0">2007 c 136 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.5.8.0">2010 c 361 art 5 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/97/#laws.0.17.0">2011 c 97 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.12.2.0">2013 c 85 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/29/#laws.0.2.0">2021 c 29 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.216B.2402">
        <h1 class="shn">216B.2402 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.16#stat.216B.16.6b">216B.16, subdivision 6b</a>, and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2401">216B.2401</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241">216B.241</a>, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Consumer-owned utility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Consumer-owned utility" means a municipal gas utility, a municipal electric utility, or a cooperative electric association.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cumulative lifetime savings.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cumulative lifetime savings" means the total electric energy or natural gas savings in a given year from energy conservation improvements installed in that given year and energy conservation improvements installed in previous years that are still in operation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Data mining facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Data mining facility" means all buildings, structures, equipment, and installations at a single site where electricity is used primarily by computers to process transactions involving digital currency that is not issued by a central authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Efficient fuel-switching improvement.</span></h2>
          <p>"Efficient fuel-switching improvement" means a project that:</p>
          <p>(1) replaces a fuel used by a customer with electricity or natural gas delivered at retail by a utility subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2403">216B.2403</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241">216B.241</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) results in a net increase in the use of electricity or natural gas and a net decrease in source energy consumption on a fuel-neutral basis;</p>
          <p>(3) otherwise meets the criteria established for consumer-owned utilities in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2403#stat.216B.2403.8">216B.2403, subdivision 8</a>, and for public utilities under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241">216B.241, subdivisions 11 </a>and 12; and</p>
          <p>(4) requires the installation of equipment that utilizes electricity or natural gas, resulting in a reduction or elimination of the previous fuel used.</p>
          <p class="bl">An efficient fuel-switching improvement is not an energy conservation improvement or energy efficiency even if the efficient fuel-switching improvement results in a net reduction in electricity or natural gas use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Energy conservation.</span></h2>
          <p>"Energy conservation" means an action that results in a net reduction in electricity or natural gas consumption. Energy conservation does not include an efficient fuel-switching improvement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Energy conservation improvement.</span></h2>
          <p>"Energy conservation improvement" means a project that results in energy efficiency or energy conservation. Energy conservation improvement may include waste heat that is recovered and converted into electricity or used as thermal energy, but does not include electric utility infrastructure projects approved by the commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.1636">216B.1636</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Energy efficiency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Energy efficiency" means measures or programs, including energy conservation measures or programs, that: (1) target consumer behavior, equipment, processes, or devices; (2) are designed to reduce the consumption of electricity or natural gas on either an absolute or per unit of production basis; and (3) do not reduce the quality or level of service provided to an energy consumer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Fuel.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fuel" means energy, including electricity, propane, natural gas, heating oil, gasoline, diesel fuel, or steam, consumed by a retail utility customer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Fuel neutral.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fuel neutral" means an approach that compares the use of various fuels for a given end use, using a common metric.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Gross annual retail energy sales.</span></h2>
          <p>"Gross annual retail energy sales" means a utility's annual electric sales to all Minnesota retail customers, or natural gas throughput to all retail customers, including natural gas transportation customers, on a utility's distribution system in Minnesota. Gross annual retail energy sales does not include:</p>
          <p>(1) gas sales to:</p>
          <p>(i) a large energy facility;</p>
          <p>(ii) a large customer facility whose natural gas utility has been exempted by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241#stat.216B.241.1a">216B.241, subdivision 1a</a>, paragraph (a), with respect to natural gas sales made to the large customer facility; and</p>
          <p>(iii) a commercial gas customer facility whose natural gas utility has been exempted by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241#stat.216B.241.1a">216B.241, subdivision 1a</a>, paragraph (b), with respect to natural gas sales made to the commercial gas customer facility;</p>
          <p>(2) electric sales to:</p>
          <p>(i) a large customer facility whose electric utility has been exempted by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241#stat.216B.241.1a">216B.241, subdivision 1a</a>, paragraph (a), with respect to electric sales made to the large customer facility; and</p>
          <p>(ii) a data mining facility, if the facility:</p>
          <p>(A) has provided a signed letter to the utility verifying the facility meets the definition of a data mining facility; and</p>
          <p>(B) imposes a peak electrical demand on a consumer-owned utility's system equal to or greater than 40 percent of the peak electrical demand of the system, measured in the same manner as the utility that serves the customer facility measures electric demand for billing purposes; or</p>
          <p>(3) the amount of electric sales prior to December 31, 2032, that are associated with a utility's program, rate, or tariff for electric vehicle charging based on a methodology and assumptions developed by the department in consultation with interested stakeholders no later than December 31, 2021. After December 31, 2032, incremental sales to electric vehicles must be included in calculating a public utility's gross annual retail sales.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Investments and expenses of a public utility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Investments and expenses of a public utility" means the investments and expenses incurred by a public utility in connection with an energy conservation improvement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Large customer facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Large customer facility" means all buildings, structures, equipment, and installations at a single site that in aggregate: (1) impose a peak electrical demand on an electric utility's system of at least 20,000 kilowatts, measured in the same way as the utility that serves the customer facility measures electric demand for billing purposes; or (2) consume at least 500,000,000 cubic feet of natural gas annually. When calculating peak electrical demand, a large customer facility may include demand offset by on-site cogeneration facilities and, if engaged in mineral extraction, may include peak energy demand from the large customer facility's mining processing operations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Large energy facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Large energy facility" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2421#stat.216B.2421.2">216B.2421, subdivision 2</a>, clause (1).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Lifetime energy savings.</span></h2>
          <p>"Lifetime energy savings" means the amount of savings a particular energy conservation improvement is projected to produce over the improvement's effective useful lifetime.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.216B.2402.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Load management.</span></h2>
          <p>"Load management" means an activity, service, or technology that changes the timing or the efficiency of a customer's use of energy that allows a utility or a customer to: (1) respond to local and regional energy system conditions; or (2) reduce peak demand for electricity or natural gas. Load management that reduces a customer's net annual energy consumption is also energy conservation.</p>
        </div>
